<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}$ and $\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {1}{3} + \dfrac {1}{2}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {1 \times 2}{3 \times 2} + \dfrac {1 \times 3}{2 \times 3}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {2}{6} + \dfrac {3}{6}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {5}{6}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {5}{12}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {5}{12}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {1}{3} + \dfrac {1}{2}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {1 \times 2}{3 \times 2} + \dfrac {1 \times 3}{2 \times 3}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {2}{6} + \dfrac {3}{6}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1}{2} \times (\dfrac {5}{6}) \\\\
&= \dfrac {5}{12}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {5}{12}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many red counters does she have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Red counters} &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 60 \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {60} {3} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {3} } {\cancel {3} } \\\\
                     &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Red counters} &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 60 \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {60} {3} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                     &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {3} } {\cancel {3} } \\\\
                     &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$25\%$ of her counters are blue.

How many blue counters does she have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Blue counters} &= 25 \% \ \text {of} \ 60 \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {25 \times 60} {100} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {25 \times 4 \times 15} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {\cancel{25} \times \cancel{4} \times 15} {\cancel{25} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                      &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Blue counters} &= 25 \% \ \text {of} \ 60 \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {25 \times 60} {100} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {25 \times 4 \times 15} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {\cancel{25} \times \cancel{4} \times 15} {\cancel{25} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                      &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

The rest of the counters are green.

How many more green counters than blue counters are there?

<div class='workings'>

\begin{aligned}

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

![the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-11-00.png](/assets/the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-11-00.png "the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-11-00.png")

Use this information to write:

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$7 \ \text{inches}$ in centimetres

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 \ \text{inches} \\\\
&= 7 \times 2.5 \ \text{centimeters} \\\\
&= 17.5 \ \text{centimeters}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17.5 \ \text{centimeters}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 7 \ \text{inches} \\\\
&= 7 \times 2.5 \ \text{centimeters} \\\\
&= 17.5 \ \text{centimeters}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17.5 \ \text{centimeters}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$70  \ \text{inches}$ in metres

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 70 \ \text{inches} \\\\
&= 70 \times 2.5 \ \text {centimeters} \\\\
&= 70 \times \dfrac {2.5} {100} \ \text {meters} \\\\
&= 70 \times 0.025 \\\\
&= 0.175 \ \text {meters}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.175 \ \text{meters}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 70 \ \text{inches} \\\\
&= 70 \times 2.5 \ \text {centimeters} \\\\
&= 70 \times \dfrac {2.5} {100} \ \text {meters} \\\\
&= 70 \times 0.025 \\\\
&= 0.175 \ \text {meters}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$0.175 \ \text{meters}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Add up all the factors and halve the total. 

Write down your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (1 + 2 + 4 + 7 + 14 + 28) \\\\
&= \dfrac {56} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 28
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (1 + 2 + 4 + 7 + 14 + 28) \\\\
&= \dfrac {56} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 28
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A perfect number is a special type of number.

If you add up all of its factors and halve the total, the result is the original number.

Find a perfect number which is less than $10$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

We can easily infer the value by using trial and error.

| Number 	| Factors 	    | Half of Sum 	                            | Perfect Number 	|
|:------:	|:-----------:	|:-------------------------------------:	|:---:	|
| $1$ 	    | $1$ 	        |  $\dfrac {1} {2}$	| No 	|
| $2$ 	    | $1, 2$ 	    |  $\dfrac {1 + 2} {2} = \dfrac {3} {2}$	| No 	|
| $3$ 	    | $1, 3$ 	    |  $\dfrac {1 + 3} {2} = 2$	                | No 	|
| $4$ 	    | $1, 2, 4$     | $1 + 2 = 4$ 	                            | No 	|
| $5$ 	    | $1, 5$ 	    |  	                                        | No 	|
| $6$ 	    | $1, 2, 3, 6$ 	| $1 + 2 + 3 = 6$ 	                        | Yes 	|

Number $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>We can easily infer the value by using trial and error.
<emptyline>
| Number 	| Factors 	    | Half of Sum 	                            | Perfect Number 	|
|:------:	|:-----------:	|:-------------------------------------:	|:---:	|
| $1$ 	    | $1$ 	        |  $\dfrac {1} {2}$	| No 	|
| $2$ 	    | $1, 2$ 	    |  $\dfrac {1 + 2} {2} = \dfrac {3} {2}$	| No 	|
| $3$ 	    | $1, 3$ 	    |  $\dfrac {1 + 3} {2} = 2$	                | No 	|
| $4$ 	    | $1, 2, 4$     | $1 + 2 = 4$ 	                            | No 	|
| $5$ 	    | $1, 5$ 	    |  	                                        | No 	|
| $6$ 	    | $1, 2, 3, 6$ 	| $1 + 2 + 3 = 6$ 	                        | Yes 	|
<emptyline>
Number $= 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 25 \ \% \ \text{of} 80 \\\\
&= \dfrac {25 \times 80} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {25 \times 8 \cancel 0}{10 \cancel 0} \\\\
&= \dfrac {25 \times 8} {10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 5 \times 2\times4} {5\times2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel{5} \times \cancel{2} \times4}{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 4 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$20$

&= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel{5} \times \cancel{2} \times4}{\cancel{5} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 5 \times 4 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=110 \div 0.2 \\\\
&=110 \div \dfrac{2}{10} \\\\
&=\dfrac{110 \times 10}{2} \\\\
&=\dfrac {110 \times \cancel {2} \times 5}{\cancel 2} \\\\
&=110 \times 5 \\\\
&=550
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

Four numbers $A, B, C$ and $D$ have been written out as a fraction, a decimal and a percentage.

Complete the table giving fractions in their simplest form.

|  	| Fraction 	| Decimal 	| Percentage 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|
| A 	| $\dfrac{1}{5}$ 	| $0.2$ 	| $20\%$ 	|
| B 	| $\dfrac{4}{5}$ 	|  	|  	|
| C 	|  	|  	| $83\%$ 	|
| D 	|  	| $0.08$ 	|  	|

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|  	| Fraction 	| Decimal 	| Percentage 	|
|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|:---:	|
| A 	| $\dfrac{1}{5}$ 	| $0.2$ 	| $20\%$ 	|
| B 	| $\dfrac{4}{5}$ 	| $0.8$ 	| $80\%$ 	|
| C 	| $\dfrac{83}{100}$ 	| $0.83$ 	| $83\%$ 	|
| D 	| $\dfrac{8}{100}$ 	| $0.08$ 	| $8\%$ 	|

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.8, 80\%, \dfrac{83}{100}, 0.83, \dfrac{8}{100}, 8\%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>|  	| Fraction 	| Decimal 	| Percentage 	|

| B 	| $\dfrac{4}{5}$ 	| $0.8$ 	| $80\%$ 	|
| C 	| $\dfrac{83}{100}$ 	| $0.83$ 	| $83\%$ 	|
| D 	| $\dfrac{8}{100}$ 	| $0.08$ 	| $8\%$ 	|
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

What is the difference, in cm, between $3.2 \ \text{metres}$ and $30 \ \text{cm}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}    &= 3.2 \ \text{m} - 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                     &= 3.2 \times 100 \ \text{cm} - 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                     &= 320 - 30   \\\\
                     &= 290  \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$290  \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}    &= 3.2 \ \text{m} - 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                     &= 3.2 \times 100 \ \text{cm} - 30 \ \text{cm} \\\\
                     &= 320 - 30   \\\\
                     &= 290  \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

&= \dfrac{15 \times 17 \times 2}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{15 \times 34}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{510}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{255 \times 2}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{255 \times \cancel {2}}{\cancel {2}} \\\\
&= 255
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

\begin{aligned}
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - 32 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - \dfrac{32}{100} \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - 0.32 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 2.38 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table of values for this number machine with the rule 'multiply by $4$, then subtract $3$'.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-13-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-13-subquestion-1-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-13-subquestion-1-00.png")

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Number IN $= 10$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 10 \times 4 - 3 \\\\
&= 40 - 3 \\\\
&= 37
\end{aligned}
$

Number OUT $= 81$

Let input number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a - 3 &= 81 \\\\
4 \times a &= 81 + 3 \\\\
4 \times a &= 84 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{84}{4} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{21 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
a &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$37 \\\\ 21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Number IN $= 10$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

&= 40 - 3 \\\\
&= 37
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a &= \dfrac{84}{4} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{21 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
a &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the rule for this number machine? Write your answer inside the machine.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-13-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-13-subquestion-2-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-13-subquestion-2-00.png")

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the rule $= \text{Multiply by}\ a \ \text{then add}\ b$

Input $= 2$

Output $= 17$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + b &= 17 \\\\
2a + b &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

Input $= 7$

Output $= 52$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 7 + b &= 52 \\\\
7a + b &= 52
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for $a$

$
\begin{aligned}
7a + b &= 52 \\\\
7a + (17 - 2a) &= 52 \\\\
7a - 2a &= 52 - 17 \\\\
5a &= 35 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{35}{5} \\\\
a &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for $b$

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + b &= 17 \\\\
2 \times 7 + b &= 17 \\\\
14 + b &= 17 \\\\
b &= 17 - 14 \\\\
b &= 3 \\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets verify,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 9 \times 7 + 3 \\\\
&= 63 + 3 \\\\
&= \green {66} = 66 \\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 10 \times 7 + 3 \\\\
&= 70 + 3 \\\\
&= \green {73} = 73 \\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= \text{Multipy by}\ 7 \ \text{then add}\ 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{Multipy by}\ 7 \ \text{then add}\ 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the rule $= \text{Multiply by}\ a \ \text{then add}\ b$
<emptyline>

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore, the rule $= \text{Multipy by}\ 7 \ \text{then add}\ 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

$5 \ \boxed{ \phantom{+} } \ 3 =  12 \ \boxed{ \phantom{+} } \   4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$5 \ \boxed{ \green{ + } } \ 3 =  12 \ \boxed{ \green{ - } } \ 4$

Lets verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Left}       &= 5 + 3 \\\\
                  &= 8 \\\\
\text{Right}      &= 12 - 4 \\\\
                  &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$2 \ \boxed{ \green{ \times } } \ 3 =  12 \ \boxed{ \green{ \div } } \ 2$

Lets verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Left}       &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                  &= 6 \\\\
\text{Right}      &= 12 \div 2 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{12}{2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{6 \times 2}{2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{6 \times \cancel {2}}{\cancel {2}} \\\\
                  &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

All the water from these two beakers is poured into the empty beaker. 
Draw a line to show the level of the water in the new beaker.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2/section-1-question-17-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2/section-1-question-17-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2/section-1-question-17-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Water in first beaker}  &= 300 \ \text{ml}
                              &= 300 \times \dfrac{1}{1000} \ \text{litre} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{300}{1000} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{100} \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {3}{10} \\\\
                              &= 0.3 \ \text{litre} \\\\
\text{Water in first beaker}  &= 450 \ \text{ml}
                              &= 450 \times \dfrac{1}{1000} \ \text{litre} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{450}{1000} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{45 \times 10}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{45 \times 10}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{45 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{10} \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {45}{100} \\\\
                              &= 0.45 \ \text{litre}   \\\\
\text{Total amount of water}  &= 0.3 + 0.45 \\\\
                              &= 0.75 \ \text{litre}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.75 \ \text{litre}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Water in first beaker}  &= 300 \ \text{ml}
                              &= 300 \times \dfrac{1}{1000} \ \text{litre} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{300}{1000} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{100} \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {3}{10} \\\\
                              &= 0.3 \ \text{litre} \\\\
\text{Water in first beaker}  &= 450 \ \text{ml}
                              &= 450 \times \dfrac{1}{1000} \ \text{litre} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{450}{1000} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{45 \times 10}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{45 \times 10}{100 \times 10} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{45 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{10} \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {45}{100} \\\\
                              &= 0.45 \ \text{litre}   \\\\
\text{Total amount of water}  &= 0.3 + 0.45 \\\\
                              &= 0.75 \ \text{litre}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>


Here are four cards which you can use to make numbers:

$
\boxed{\huge { \text{ 1 }}} \quad
\boxed{\huge { \text{ 2 }}} \quad
\boxed{\huge { \text{ 3 }}} \quad
\boxed{\huge { \text{ 4 }}}
$

Write down all the possible $4$-digit even numbers you can make which are greater than $3000$.
Write your answers from smallest to biggest.

</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Which of the following numbers is closest in value to $1$?

$1.1, \quad 0.988, \quad 1.009, \quad 0.99, \quad 1.01$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's calculate the difference between the numbers and $1$.

| Number   |     Difference       |
|:--------:|:--------------------:|
|   $1.1$  |  $1.1 - 1 = 0.1$   |
|  $0.988$ | $1 - 0.988 = 0.012$  |
|  $1.009$ | $1.009 - 1 = 0.009$  |
|  $0.99$  |  $1 - 0.99 = 0.01$   |
|  $1.01$  |  $1.01 - 1 = 0.01$   |

Smallest difference $= 0.009$

Number closest in value $= 1.009$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.009$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's calculate the difference between the numbers and $1$.
<emptyline>
| Number   |     Difference       |
|:--------:|:--------------------:|
|   $1.1$  |  $1.1 - 1 = 0.1$   |
|  $0.988$ | $1 - 0.988 = 0.012$  |
|  $1.009$ | $1.009 - 1 = 0.009$  |
|  $0.99$  |  $1 - 0.99 = 0.01$   |
|  $1.01$  |  $1.01 - 1 = 0.01$   |
<emptyline>
Smallest difference $= 0.009$
<emptyline>
Number closest in value $= 1.009$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.009$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

$9 \ \boxed{\phantom{555}} \ 4 = 25\ \boxed{\phantom{555}} \  5$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$9 \ \boxed{-} \ 4 = 25\ \boxed{\div} \  5$

Lets verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Left}       &= 9 - 4 \\\\
                  &= 5 \\\\
\text{Right}      &= 25 \div 5 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{25}{5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{5 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 5}{\cancel 5} \\\\
                  &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-, \div$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9 \ \boxed{-} \ 4 = 25\ \boxed{\div} \  5$
<emptyline>

                  &= 5 \\\\

                  &= \dfrac{25}{5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{5 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 5}{\cancel 5} \\\\
                  &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

\begin{aligned}

&= 7
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

$32 \div ( 10 - \boxed{\phantom{5555}}) = 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
32 \div (10 - a)        &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac{32}{10 - a}      &= 4 \\\\
10 - a                  &= \dfrac{32}{4} \\\\
10 - a                  &= 8 \\\\
a                       &= 10 - 8 \\\\
a                       &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
